The paper discusses how neural alignment is done across mice, but I ignored this to reduce complexity. \n\nii. \n```python\ndef get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n    # parse sessions from files named sub-<subject>-<session>_behavior+ophys.nwb\n    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n    return sessions\n...\nsessions_per_subject = {subject: get_sessions(subject, datadir=args.datadir) for subject in data['subjects']}\n```\n\niii. The start of each trial is identified based on the `behavior` variable `trial_start`. The end of each trial is identified by the `teleport` variable. I grab out data between these time points to correspond to trials. \n\nii. \n```python\ndef get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start,teleport):\n    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1 \n    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx), f\"Number of trial starts ({len(trial_start_idx)}) does not match number of trial ends ({len(trial_end_idx)})\"\n    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n...\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n...\nteleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:]\ntrial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], teleport)\n```\n\niii. I investigated using the `trial_number` variable, but led to visually less clean data. The lengths and numbers of trials were consistent with this choice, and it matched the definition of a trial in the paper. \n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls? \n\ni. Trials with `< 50` time points were removed. \n\nii. \n```python\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n        print(f\"Skipping trial {trial} in subject {subject} session {session} because it has only {idx.sum() < {min_ntimepoints} time points\")\n        istrial[trial] = False\n        continue\n```\n\niii. Looking at trial lengths, there were a couple short trials that this removed, at some version of the code. \n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the final `neural` data derived from? \n\ni. Neural data is derived from the raw suite2p traces, `Fluorescence` (F) and `Neuropil` (Fneu). It is **not** the NWB's `Deconvolved` field, which is a different signal that the paper never analyzes.\n\nii. \n```python\nfluorescence_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Fluorescence\"]\nneuropil_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Neuropil\"]\nfor plane_name, plane_data in fluorescence_data.roi_response_series.items():\n    fluorescence_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n    neuropil_curr = neuropil_data.roi_response_series[plane_name].data[:]\n```\n\niii. The paper computes its own neural signal rather than using a stored one in `preprocessing.dff(F, Fneu, ..., deconvolve=True)` and stores the result as `sess.timeseries['events']`. The NWB's `Deconvolved` array is instead suite2p's own deconvolution of raw fluorescence.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed? \n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu and then deconvolved into \"events\", by a copy of the paper's own `preprocessing.dff`. Per plane: restrict to the lap samples (or lap plus teleport, depending on the session), subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, add each trial's mean neuropil back so the ratio is a true dF/F, take a maximin baseline (Gaussian smoothing with sigma 15 samples, then a 300-sample running minimum followed by a 300-sample running maximum, the Methods' 20 s window), form `(F - baseline)/|baseline|`, smooth with a 2-sample Gaussian, and deconvolve with OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate/n_planes`. Cells from multiple planes are pooled afterwards.\n\nWhether the baseline window may span the teleport is a per-mouse, per-day fact, copied from `teleport_metadata.py`: the laser was blanked between trials except on the days listed there, where imaging continued. It matters -- on m15 day 5, spanning the teleport widens the baseline window by 35% and moves the emitted events by ~9% (median per-cell r = 0.986).\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                            f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                            subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n                            frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n                            keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n```\n\niii. This is the processing the Methods describe: \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window ... dF/F was then calculated for each cell as the fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute value of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\" The parameters are theirs: `neu_coef = 0.7` and `baseline_method = 'maximin'` from `notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`, `tau = 0.7` from the suite2p ops in `notebooks/suite2p_notebooks/example_m12.ipynb`.\n\nThe neural sampling rate is the same for all recordings once the multi-plane sessions are accounted for. Note that the `rate` stored on the NWB series is the scanner rate, so on the two-plane sessions it is 31 Hz and the per-plane rate -- the one that sets both the time bin and the deconvolution kernel -- is `rate/nplanes`.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters, both the paper's. Cells are first restricted to the `iscell` ROIs, which carry suite2p's manual curation. Putative interneurons are then dropped: any cell whose dF/F correlates with the animal's running speed at r > 0.5.\n\nii. \n```python\niscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\nfluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\nneuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n...\nisint = is_putative_interneuron(dff_curr, speed)\nspike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n```\n\niii. `iscell` is the manual curation the Methods describe. The per-session counts approximately matches ranges in the paper. The second filter is the Methods' additional step of filtering putative interneurons. \n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`? \n\ni. The data are the be aligned with the trial start, which doesn't require any additional processing beyond splitting into trials. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 2-e. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n\ni. The neural data is not binned or resampled, it is kept at the stored rate. \n\nii. \n```python\nneural_time_bin_size = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n...\nassert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Neural time bin size ({neural_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n```\n\niii. Timestamps are not available for the neural data, just a stored `rate`. This rate is the same for all recordings, once one accounts for the multi-plane recordings\n\n## 3-a. This input is derived from the `timestamps` variable that is available for every behavior time series. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward event timestamps are matched to behavior timestamps, and a binary `isreward` array marks timepoints where rewards occurred.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\nreward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n```\n\niii. The `Reward` time series has its own timestamps (not aligned to the behavior sampling rate), so `searchsorted` is used to find the nearest timepoint. An assertion checks that the alignment error is within half a time bin.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial, check whether any reward event occurred in the *previous* trial's time range. For the first trial, set to 0. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nif trial == 0:\n    input_curr[3,:] = 0\nelse:\n    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \nimport pynwb\nimport os\nimport warnings\nimport numpy as np\nimport matplotlib\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport pickle\nimport argparse\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy.interpolate import interp1d\nfrom scipy import ndimage\nfrom suite2p.extraction import dcnv\n\nDATADIR = 'data'\nSURVEYFILE = 'survey_info.pkl'\n\n_sosa2024_seed = 'x5cidj2hy87s'\nnp.random.seed(sum(ord(c) for c in _sosa2024_seed) % 2**31)\n\ninput_names = ['time_from_trial_start','environment','trial_number','previous_reward_outcome']\noutput_names = ['distance_to_reward_zone','position','speed','lick','reward_location','reward_outcome']\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\nspeed_bins = [-np.inf, 2, 10, 20, 40, np.inf]\noutput_values = [\n    [\n        '< -50 cm',\n        '-50 to -10 cm',\n        '-10 cm to < 0 cm',\n        '0 cm',\n        '>0 cm to +10 cm',\n        '+10 to +50 cm',\n        '> +50 cm'\n    ],\n    [\n        '0 to 90 cm',\n        '90 to 180 cm',\n        '180 to 270 cm',\n        '270 to 360 cm',\n        '360 to 450 cm',\n    ],\n    [\n        '< 2 cm/s',\n        '2 to 10 cm/s',\n        '10 to 20 cm/s',\n        '20 to 40 cm/s',\n        '> 40 cm/s',\n    ],\n    ['no', 'yes'],\n    ['A', 'B', 'C'],\n    ['no','yes']\n]\n\n# inspired by Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n\n# =================================================================================================\n# The paper's neural signal processing, COPIED from the paper's code repo\n#\n# The NWB \"Deconvolved\" array is NOT the signal the paper analyses. find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = .001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n\n    out = a_nanless / one\n    if return_nans:\n        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n\n    return out\n\ndef dff(f,\n        trial_starts,\n        teleports,\n        f_neu=None,\n        neuropil_method=None,\n        baseline_method='maximin',\n        subtract_baseline=True,\n        neu_coef=0.7,\n        tau=0.7,\n        frame_rate=15,\n        n_planes=1,\n        deconvolve=False,\n        keep_teleports=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n\n    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n    :param trial_starts:\n    :param teleports:\n    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n    :param baseline_method:\n    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n    :param neu_coef:\n    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n\n    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n    \"\"\"\n\n    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n\n    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n    if deconvolve:\n        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n            warnings.warn(\n                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n\n    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if f_neu is not None:\n        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if keep_teleports:\n        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n        start_inds = np.append(\n            trial_starts[0],\n            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n        ).tolist()\n        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n    else:\n        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        f_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n        if f_neu is not None:\n            f_neu_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f_neu[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    # once bleedthrough is subtracted, do neuropil correction\n\n    if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n        f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n\n    print('No minimum subtraction')\n\n    # Caluclate baseline for chan 1\n    flow = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    spks = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n\n    # get baseline within each trial\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n\n        # if we subtracted neuropil, add back in the neuropil mean in each trial\n        # such that dff values will be close to true ∆F/F, and we don't divide by small numbers\n        if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n            f_[:, start-1:stop-1] = f_[:, start-1:stop-1] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(\n                f_neu_[:, start-1:stop-1], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n\n        if baseline_method == 'maximin':\n            # cut out ITIs and smooth signal\n            flow[:, start - 1:stop -\n                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n\n            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n\n    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    if subtract_baseline:\n        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n\n    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n        if deconvolve:\n            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n\n    if deconvolve:\n        return dff, spks\n    else:\n        return dff\n\n\ndef is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n\n    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n    \"\"\"\n    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n\n    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n\n    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n\n    return is_int\n\n# end copy-paste\n# =================================================================================================\n\n\ndef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n    # find all directories in datadir that start with 'sub-'\n    subjects = [d.split('-')[1] for d in os.listdir(datadir) if d.startswith('sub-')]\n    return subjects\n\ndef get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n    # parse sessions from files named sub-<subject>-<session>_behavior+ophys.nwb\n    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n    return sessions\n\ndef get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n    nwb_file_path = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\", f\"sub-{subject}_ses-{session:02d}_behavior+ophys.nwb\")\n    assert os.path.exists(nwb_file_path), f\"File {nwb_file_path} does not exist\"\n    return nwb_file_path\n\ndef interp(new_idx,old_idx,old_data,**kwargs):\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

How is the `neural` data processed? \n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:119:i. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu and then deconvolved into \"events\", by a copy of the paper's own `preprocessing.dff`. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:298:i. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:338:i. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:529:- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:530:- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:546:iii. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:552:2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:560:## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:562:i. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:568:## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:570:i. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n/tests/reference_DECISIONS.md:576:## 13-d. Five of six exceed 1.5x chance. `reward_outcome` (0.574 vs 0.5) is the exception and was investigated:\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:405:- **Probe (`/app/cache/reward_probe.py`)**: an independent per-session logistic regression (5-fold stratified CV, balanced accuracy) on trial-averaged event rates gives 0.49-0.65 depending on session, i.e. the same ceiling the neural decoder reaches. Restricting to frames **after** the reward zone start gives the same or slightly better accuracy (0.49-0.66), while frames **before** the zone give chance (0.49-0.53). This is exactly the expected structure: whether a trial is rewarded is simply not knowable from CA1 activity before the animal reaches the zone, yet the per-trial label is (by specification) broadcast over the whole trial, so most timepoints are unpredictable in principle. It also rules out label leakage.\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:406:- The paper itself reports only a modest reward-versus-omission signal (Fig. 6: reward-vs-omission indices near 0 for most cells), so a low but above-chance accuracy is the biologically expected result.\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:410:The paper's only decoding analysis (Fig. 3) is a **circular-linear regression of reward-relative position** scored with the mean cosine error, not a classification accuracy, so no number is directly comparable. The qualitative comparison:\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:413:| Reward-relative (distance to zone) position | 0.550 (7 classes, chance 0.143) | decode score well above shuffle, informative over roughly -105 to +153 cm around the reward (Fig. 3b,c) |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:414:| Absolute track position | 0.671 (5 classes, chance 0.200) | strong spatial coding: 459 +- 263 place cells per session (~50% of cells) |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:415:| Speed / licking | 0.596 / 0.746 | movement variables are significant GLM predictors (Fig. 7) |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:417:| Reward outcome | 0.574 | small reward-vs-omission index for most cells (Fig. 6) |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:418:My accuracies are consistent with, or better than, what the paper's effect sizes imply; the strongest decoding is for the spatial/reward-zone variables, exactly as reported.\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:423:| dist_to_reward_zone | 0.625 | 0.550 | 1.14 |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:424:| position | 0.725 | 0.671 | 1.08 |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:425:| speed | 0.640 | 0.596 | 1.07 |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:426:| lick | 0.766 | 0.746 | 1.03 |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:427:| reward_zone_location | 0.900 | 0.848 | 1.06 |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:428:| reward_outcome | 0.813 | 0.574 | **1.42** |\n/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md:429:Only `reward_outcome` shows a substantial gap (still < 1.5x). {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_3","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,620p' /app/convert_data.py && sed -n '1,320p' /app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md\"","aggregated_output":"\"\"\"\nConvert Sosa, Plitt & Giocomo (2025) hippocampal CA1 2-photon VR dataset (DANDI 001361)\ninto the decoder-ready pickle format.\n\nUsage:\n    python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\nProcessing follows the reference code (github.com/GiocomoLab/Sosa_et_al_2024):\n  * trials = frames between `trial_start` and `teleport` (teleport/ITI excluded)\n  * dF/F   = neuropil subtraction (0.7) with per-trial neuropil mean added back,\n             maximin baseline (gaussian sigma 15 frames -> 300-frame min -> 300-frame max),\n             dF/F = (F - F0) / |F0|, gaussian smoothing with 2-frame s.d.\n             (reward_relative/preprocessing.py :: dff)\n  * events = OASIS deconvolution of dF/F (suite2p dcnv.oasis, tau = 0.7)\n  * cells  = suite2p iscell == 1, minus putative interneurons (corr(dF/F, speed) > 0.5)\n  * trials with lick-sensor errors (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) dropped\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport re\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport pickle\nimport argparse\nimport warnings\nfrom glob import glob\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport scipy.ndimage as ndi\n\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n\nDATA_ROOT = '/app/data'\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- constants\nNEU_COEF = 0.7            # neuropil coefficient (reference default)\nBASELINE_WIN = 300        # frames (~19.3 s ~ 20 s) for maximin min/max filters\nBASELINE_SMOOTH = 15      # gaussian sigma (frames) before maximin\nDFF_SMOOTH = 2            # gaussian sigma (frames) applied to dF/F\nTAU = 0.7                 # GCaMP7f decay constant used by the reference suite2p ops\nINTERNEURON_R = 0.5       # dF/F vs speed correlation threshold for interneuron exclusion\nLICK_ERROR_FRAC = 0.30    # >30% of frames with cumulative lick > 2 => lick sensor error\nLICK_ERROR_COUNT = 2\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450.0\nREWARD_ZONES = {'A': (80.0, 130.0), 'B': (200.0, 250.0), 'C': (320.0, 370.0)}\nZONE_TO_IDX = {'A': 0, 'B': 1, 'C': 2}\nCHANGE_TRIAL = 30         # reward zone switches on trial index 30 (31st trial)\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_trial_start_s', 'environment', 'trial_number', 'prev_trial_rewarded']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['dist_to_reward_zone', 'position', 'speed', 'lick',\n                'reward_zone_location', 'reward_outcome']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['< -50 cm', '-50 to -10 cm', '-10 to 0 cm', '0 cm (in zone)',\n     '0 to +10 cm', '+10 to +50 cm', '> +50 cm'],\n    ['< 90 cm', '90-180 cm', '180-270 cm', '270-360 cm', '> 360 cm'],\n    ['< 2 cm/s', '2-10 cm/s', '10-20 cm/s', '20-40 cm/s', '> 40 cm/s'],\n    ['no lick', 'lick'],\n    ['A (80-130 cm)', 'B (200-250 cm)', 'C (320-370 cm)'],\n    ['omitted', 'rewarded'],\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- helpers\ndef nansmooth1d(a, sig, axis=-1):\n    \"\"\"Gaussian smoothing that ignores NaNs (reward_relative.utilities.nansmooth).\"\"\"\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_ = np.where(nan_inds, 0.0, a)\n    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_ = ndi.gaussian_filter1d(a_, sig, axis=axis)\n    one = ndi.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\n    return a_ / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_nd(a, sig):\n    \"\"\"N-d version used by the reference for the baseline (TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth).\"\"\"\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_ = np.where(nan_inds, 0.0, a)\n    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_ = ndi.gaussian_filter(a_, sig)\n    one = ndi.gaussian_filter(one, sig)\n    return a_ / one\n\n\ndef scene_reward_zones(scene, ntrials, change_trial=CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    \"\"\"Per-trial reward zone label from the VR scene name.\n\n    Mirrors reward_relative.behavior.get_reward_zones: fixed-zone scenes keep one\n    zone for the whole session; switch scenes (``..._X_to_..._Y``) use zone X for the\n    first `change_trial` trials and zone Y afterwards.\n    \"\"\"\n    m = re.search(r'^Env\\d_Location([ABC])$', scene)\n    if m:\n        return np.array([m.group(1)] * ntrials)\n    m = re.search(r'([ABC])_to_(?:Env\\d_)?(?:Location)?([ABC])$', scene)\n    if m:\n        n0 = min(change_trial, ntrials)\n        return np.array([m.group(1)] * n0 + [m.group(2)] * (ntrials - n0))\n    raise NotImplementedError(f'Reward zones not defined for scene {scene}')\n\n\ndef empirical_reward_zones(pos, rzone, starts, teles):\n    \"\"\"Zone label per trial inferred from the animal position at reward-zone entry.\n\n    Returns an array with '?' for trials in which the zone was never triggered\n    (omission trials).\n    \"\"\"\n    starts_cm = np.array([REWARD_ZONES[z][0] for z in 'ABC'])\n    labels = []\n    for a, b in zip(starts, teles):\n        idx = np.where(rzone[a:b] > 0)[0]\n        if len(idx) == 0:\n            labels.append('?')\n        else:\n            p = pos[a:b][idx[0]]\n            labels.append('ABC'[int(np.argmin(np.abs(starts_cm - p)))])\n    return np.array(labels)\n\n\ndef discretize_distance(d):\n    \"\"\"Distance (cm) to the reward zone -> 7 categories (see OUTPUT_VALUES[0]).\"\"\"\n    out = np.full(d.shape, 3, dtype=np.int8)        # 3 == inside the zone (d == 0)\n    out[d < -50] = 0\n    out[(d >= -50) & (d < -10)] = 1\n    out[(d >= -10) & (d < 0)] = 2\n    out[(d > 0) & (d <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(d > 10) & (d <= 50)] = 5\n    out[d > 50] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef discretize_position(pos):\n    \"\"\"Position (cm) -> 5 equal bins spanning the 450 cm track.\"\"\"\n    p = np.clip(pos, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH - 1e-9)\n    return np.floor(p / 90.0).astype(np.int8)\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    return np.digitize(speed, [2.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0]).astype(np.int8)\n\n\ndef signed_distance_to_zone(pos, zone_start, zone_end):\n    \"\"\"Signed distance (cm) to the nearest point of the reward zone; 0 inside.\"\"\"\n    d = np.zeros_like(pos)\n    before = pos < zone_start\n    after = pos > zone_end\n    d[before] = pos[before] - zone_start\n    d[after] = pos[after] - zone_end\n    return d\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- loading\ndef load_session(fn):\n    \"\"\"Load one NWB session with pynwb; returns a dict of raw arrays and metadata.\"\"\"\n    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\n    with NWBHDF5IO(fn, 'r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n        nwb = io.read()\n        subject = nwb.subject.subject_id\n        scene = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-1]\n        date = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-2]\n\n        beh = nwb.processing['behavior'].data_interfaces['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n        t = np.asarray(beh['position'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        pos = np.asarray(beh['position'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        speed = np.asarray(beh['speed'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        lick = np.asarray(beh['lick'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        rzone = np.asarray(beh['reward_zone'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        env = np.asarray(beh['environment'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        trial_start = np.asarray(beh['trial_start'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        teleport = np.asarray(beh['teleport'].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        reward_t = np.asarray(beh['Reward'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n\n        oph = nwb.processing['ophys'].data_interfaces\n        Fseries = oph['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series\n        Nseries = oph['Neuropil'].roi_response_series\n        plane_keys = sorted(Fseries.keys())\n        nframes = len(t)\n        F = np.concatenate([np.asarray(Fseries[k].data[:nframes, :], dtype=np.float32).T\n                            for k in plane_keys], axis=0)\n        Fneu = np.concatenate([np.asarray(Nseries[k].data[:nframes, :], dtype=np.float32).T\n                               for k in plane_keys], axis=0)\n        rate = float(Fseries[plane_keys[0]].rate)\n        n_planes = len(plane_keys)\n\n        ps = oph['ImageSegmentation'].plane_segmentations['PlaneSegmentation']\n        iscell = np.asarray(ps['iscell'].data[:])[:, 0] == 1\n        plane_idx = np.asarray(ps['planeIdx'].data[:]).astype(int)\n\n    starts = np.where(trial_start > 0)[0]\n    teles = np.where(teleport > 0)[0]\n    assert len(starts) == len(teles) and np.all(teles > starts), f'bad trial indices in {fn}'\n\n    # reward delivery as a binary frame series (Reward has timestamps only)\n    reward = np.zeros(nframes, dtype=np.float64)\n    if len(reward_t):\n        ridx = np.searchsorted(t, reward_t)\n        ridx = np.clip(ridx, 0, nframes - 1)\n        reward[ridx] = 1.0\n\n    return dict(file=os.path.basename(fn), subject=subject, scene=scene, date=date,\n                t=t, pos=pos, speed=speed, lick=lick, rzone=rzone, env=env,\n                reward=reward, starts=starts, teles=teles, F=F, Fneu=Fneu,\n                iscell=iscell, plane_idx=plane_idx, rate=rate, n_planes=n_planes)\n\n\ndef compute_dff_events(F, Fneu, starts, teles, fs):\n    \"\"\"dF/F and deconvolved events following reward_relative.preprocessing.dff.\n\n    Frames outside of trials stay NaN (the reference's keep_teleports=False).\n    \"\"\"\n    from suite2p.extraction import dcnv\n\n    f_ = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    fneu_ = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for s, e in zip(starts, teles):\n        f_[:, s:e] = F[:, s:e]\n        fneu_[:, s:e] = Fneu[:, s:e]\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    f_ -= NEU_COEF * fneu_                                  # neuropil subtraction\n\n    flow = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for s, e in zip(starts, teles):\n        # add back the trial-mean neuropil so dF/F is not divided by tiny numbers\n        f_[:, s:e] += NEU_COEF * np.nanmean(fneu_[:, s:e], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        seg = nansmooth_nd(f_[:, s:e], [0, BASELINE_SMOOTH])\n        seg = ndi.minimum_filter1d(seg, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        seg = ndi.maximum_filter1d(seg, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = seg\n\n    dff = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    events = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for s, e in zip(starts, teles):\n        dff[:, s:e] = nansmooth1d(dff[:, s:e], DFF_SMOOTH, axis=1)\n        events[:, s:e] = dcnv.oasis(np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, s:e]), 2000, TAU, fs)\n\n    return dff, events\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- per session\ndef process_session(fn, show_processing=False, plot_dir='/app'):\n    t0 = time.time()\n    S = load_session(fn)\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\n\n    starts, teles = S['starts'], S['teles']\n    ntrials_raw = len(starts)\n    fs = S['rate'] / S['n_planes']\n\n    # ---- neurons: suite2p curation\n    keep = np.where(S['iscell'])[0]\n    F = S['F'][keep]\n    Fneu = S['Fneu'][keep]\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    dff, events = compute_dff_events(F, Fneu, starts, teles, fs)\n    t_dff = time.time() - t0\n\n    # ---- neurons: exclude putative interneurons (corr(dF/F, speed) > 0.5)\n    onmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n    sp = S['speed'][onmask]\n    D = dff[:, onmask]\n    Dz = D - D.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    spz = sp - sp.mean()\n    denom = (np.sqrt((Dz ** 2).sum(axis=1)) * np.sqrt((spz ** 2).sum()))\n    corr_speed = np.where(denom > 0, (Dz @ spz) / np.maximum(denom, 1e-12), 0.0)\n    is_interneuron = corr_speed > INTERNEURON_R\n    cells = np.where(~is_interneuron)[0]\n    n_interneurons = int(is_interneuron.sum())\n\n    # ---- per-trial behaviour variables\n    pos, speed, lick = S['pos'], S['speed'], S['lick']\n    rzone, env, reward, t = S['rzone'], S['env'], S['reward'], S['t']\n\n    zone_labels = scene_reward_zones(S['scene'], ntrials_raw)\n    emp = empirical_reward_zones(pos, rzone, starts, teles)\n    valid = emp != '?'\n    zone_agree = float(np.mean(emp[valid] == zone_labels[valid])) if valid.any() else np.nan\n    if zone_agree < 1.0:\n        print(f\"  WARNING {S['file']}: scene-derived zones disagree with observed zone \"\n              f\"entries on {(1 - zone_agree) * 100:.1f}% of trials -- using observed zones\")\n        fixed = zone_labels.copy()\n        fixed[valid] = emp[valid]\n        zone_labels = fixed\n\n    isreward = np.array([bool((reward[a:b] > 0).any() and (rzone[a:b] > 0).any())\n                         for a, b in zip(starts, teles)])\n    morph = np.array([np.unique(env[a:b])[0] for a, b in zip(starts, teles)])\n    assert np.all(np.isin(morph, [0.0, 1.0])), f\"unexpected environment values in {S['file']}\"\n\n    # lick-sensor error trials (paper: 81/12,376 trials removed)\n    lick_err = np.array([(lick[a:b] > LICK_ERROR_COUNT).mean() > LICK_ERROR_FRAC\n                         for a, b in zip(starts, teles)])\n\n    # ---- build per-trial arrays\n    neural, inputs, outputs = [], [], []\n    trial_ids = []\n    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(zip(starts, teles)):\n        if lick_err[i]:\n            continue\n        sl = slice(a, b)\n        T = b - a\n        tt = (t[sl] - t[a]).astype(np.float32)\n        zl = zone_labels[i]\n        z0, z1 = REWARD_ZONES[zl]\n        d = signed_distance_to_zone(pos[sl], z0, z1)\n\n        inp = np.empty((4, T), dtype=np.float32)\n        inp[0] = tt\n        inp[1] = morph[i]\n        inp[2] = i\n        inp[3] = 1.0 if i == 0 else float(isreward[i - 1])\n\n        out = np.empty((6, T), dtype=np.int8)\n        out[0] = discretize_distance(d)\n        out[1] = discretize_position(pos[sl])\n        out[2] = discretize_speed(speed[sl])\n        out[3] = (lick[sl] > 0).astype(np.int8)\n        out[4] = ZONE_TO_IDX[zl]\n        out[5] = int(isreward[i])\n\n        neu = events[np.ix_(cells, np.arange(a, b))].astype(np.float32)\n        assert not np.isnan(neu).any(), f\"NaNs in neural data, trial {i} of {S['file']}\"\n        neural.append(neu)\n        inputs.append(inp)\n        outputs.append(out)\n        trial_ids.append(i)\n\n    info = dict(file=S['file'], subject=S['subject'], scene=S['scene'], date=S['date'],\n                n_trials_raw=ntrials_raw, n_trials=len(neural),\n                n_lick_error_trials=int(lick_err.sum()),\n                n_rois=int(len(S['iscell'])), n_iscell=int(S['iscell'].sum()),\n                n_interneurons=n_interneurons, n_neurons=len(cells),\n                n_planes=S['n_planes'], frame_rate=fs,\n                frac_rewarded=float(isreward.mean()), zone_agreement=zone_agree,\n                zone_labels=''.join(zone_labels), trial_ids=trial_ids,\n                t_load=t_load, t_dff=t_dff)\n\n    if show_processing:\n        plot_processing(S, dff, events, cells, zone_labels, isreward, lick_err,\n                        neural, inputs, outputs, plot_dir)\n\n    return dict(neural=neural, input=inputs, output=outputs,\n                subject=S['subject'], n_neurons=len(cells), info=info)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- plotting\ndef plot_processing(S, dff, events, cells, zone_labels, isreward, lick_err,\n                    neural, inputs, outputs, plot_dir):\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    sid = S['file'].replace('_behavior+ophys.nwb', '')\n    starts, teles = S['starts'], S['teles']\n    t = S['t']\n    tr0, tr1 = 0, min(6, len(starts))\n    a, b = starts[tr0], teles[tr1 - 1]\n\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(8, 1, figsize=(15, 20), ax.plot(t[a:b], events[c, a:b], lw=0.6)\n    ax.set_ylabel('events\\n(OASIS)')\n\n    ax = axes[4]\n    ax.plot(t[a:b], S['pos'][a:b], 'k-', lw=1)\n    for i in range(tr0, tr1):\n        z0, z1 = (cm)\\nzone shaded')\n\n    ax = axes[5]\n    # distance to reward zone and its discretization\n    dd = np.full(b - a, np.nan)\n    for i in range(tr0, tr1):\n        s_, e_ = starts[i], teles[i]\n ax.plot(t[a:b], S['lick'][a:b], 'k-', lw=0.8, label='cum lick/frame')\n    ax.plot(t[a:b], (S['lick'][a:b] > 0).astype(float), 'r-', lw=0.8, alpha=0.6, label='lick output')\n    ax.plot(t[a:b], S['reward'][a:b], 'g-', lw=1.2, label='reward')\n    ax.legend(fontsize=7)\n    ax.set_ylabel('licks / reward')\n    ax.set_xlabel('session time (s)')\n\n    for ax in axes:\n        for i in range(tr0, tr1):\n            ax.axvline(t[starts[i]], color='b', lw=0.8, alpha=0.6)\n            ax.axvline(t[teles[i] - 1], color='m', lw=0.8, alpha=0.6)\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig(os.path.join(plot_dir, f'processing_{sid}.png'), dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n    # ---- second figure: trial-aligned summary of converted arrays\n    ntr = len(neural)\n    maxT = max(o.shape[1] for o in outputs)\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 4, figsize=(20, 9))\n    titles = ['dist_to_reward_zone', 'position', 'speed', 'lick']\n    for k in range(4):\n        M = np.full((ntr, maxT), np.nan)\n        for i, o in enumerate(outputs):\n            M[i, :o.shape[1]] = o[k]\n        im = axes[0, k].imshow(M, aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest', origin='lower')\n        axes[0, k].set_title(titles[k] + ' (trial x time)')\n        axes[0, k].set_xlabel('frame from trial start')\n        axes[0, k].set_ylabel('trial')\n        plt.colorbar(im, ax=axes[0, k])\n\n    per_trial = np.array([[o[4, 0], o[5, 0]] for o in outputs])\n    axes[1, 0].plot(per_trial[:, 0], 'o-', ms=3)\n    axes[1, 0].set_title('reward_zone_location (0=A,1=B,2=C)')\n    axes[1, axes[1, 2].set_title('per-trial inputs')\n    axes[1, 2].set_xlabel('trial')\n    mean_act = np.array([n.mean() for n in neural])\n    axes[1, 3].plot(mean_act, 'o-', ms=3)\n    axes[1,    fig.savefig(os.path.join(plot_dir, f'processing_{sid}_trials.png'), dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- driver\ndef _worker(args):\n    fn, show, plot_dir = args\n    try:\n        res = process_session(fn, show_processing=show, plot_dir=plot_dir)\n    except Exception as e:  # keep going, report at the end\n        import    for i, job in enumerate(jobs):\n            res = _worker(job)\n            results.append(res)\n            inf = res.get('info')\n            if inf:\n min')\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n# Dataset Conversion Notes\n\n## Overview\n- **Dataset**: \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" (NWB files in /app/data)\n- **Date started**: (session start)\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents of /app:\n- `.manifest`, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yaml`\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` (this file)\n- `code/` - reference code repo (LICENSE, README.md, docs/, environments/, notebooks/, setup.py, src/)\n- `data/` - dandiset.yaml + 11 subject dirs: sub-m3, m4, m7, m11, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19\n- `decoder.py`, `train_decoder.py` - decoder reference code\n- `methods.txt`, `paper.pdf` - reference text\n- `pynwb_docs/`\n\nEnvironment verified: python3 with numpy 2.4.4, torch 2.6.0+cu124, pynwb 4.1.0.\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nRepo = `Sosa_et_al_2024` (Sosa, Plitt, Giocomo 2025, Nat Neurosci). 2-photon Ca imaging in dorsal CA1 during a VR linear track task with moving hidden reward zones.\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `create_sess` | src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py | LOADING | Builds TwoPUtils `sess` object: suite2p F/Fneu + VR sqlite aligned to imaging frames |\n| `TwoPUtils.preprocessing.vr_align_to_2P` | (TwoPUtils repo, not included) | LOADING/ALIGN | Interpolates VR behavior onto ~15.5 Hz imaging frame times -> `sess.vr_data` |\n| `dff` | src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py | PROCESSING | dF/F: neuropil subtraction (`neu_coef=0.7`), `maximin` baseline, optional deconvolution into `events` (suite2p `dcnv`, tau from s2p ops). Only frames between trial_start-1 and teleport-1 are kept (rest NaN) unless `keep_teleports` |\n| `multi_anim_sess` | src/reward_relative/utilities.py | PROCESSING/CURATION | Per experiment-day, per animal: loads sess, computes dFF (+events), place cells (100 perms, p<0.05, speed_thr 2 cm/s), and behavior info (isreward, morph, rzone, rz label, trial dict) |\n| `get_trial_types` | src/reward_relative/behavior.py | PROCESSING | Per trial: `isreward` = reward delivered AND rzone entered; `morph` = env identity (0=Env1, 1=Env2) |\n| `get_reward_zones` | src/reward_relative/behavior.py | PROCESSING | Per-trial reward zone [start,stop] cm and label. Zones: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (dict keys X/Y/Z); on switch scenes (`X_to_Y`) zone changes at trial 30 (`sess.change_reward_trial` if present) |\n| `define_trial_subsets` | src/reward_relative/behavior.py | CURATION | set0/set1 trial splits by reward zone (pre/post switch) |\n| `correct_lick_sensor_error` | src/reward_relative/behavior.py | CURATION | Trials where >thr (0.35-0.5) fraction of samples have cumulative lick count >2 => licks set to NaN (stuck sensor) |\n| `lickrate`, `frametime` | behavior.py | PROCESSING | licks binarized (`licks>0 -> 1`), divided by frame time, smoothed (gauss sigma 2 frames) |\n| `create_design_matrix` | src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py | PROCESSING | GLM predictors: rel_pos (distance to reward, circular or linear), pos, trial id, speed, accel, licks (binarized, sensor-error-corrected at 0.35), rewarded/omission state; speed threshold 2 cm/s used to mask samples |\n| spatial trial matrices | spatial.py / TwoPUtils | PROCESSING | Position bins of 10 cm from 0 to 450 cm (track length 450 cm, teleport zone excluded) |\n| `decode.py` | src/reward_relative | ANALYSIS | Low & Williams circular position decoder (regression on circular position) used in Fig 3 |\n\n### Notes\n- Neural data are **2P calcium imaging**, sampled at ~15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms/frame). Reference analyses use **deconvolved `events`** computed from dF/F for place-cell / GLM analyses, and `dff` for some plots.\n- dF/F is NOT stored in raw sess; it is computed from F and Fneu. In the NWB release the dF/F and/or deconvolved traces may already be provided (to check in Step 2).\n- Cell curation is done in the suite2p GUI (`iscell.npy`); only ROIs marked iscell are used.\n- Trials are defined by `trial_start_inds` (track entry) and `teleport_inds` (track exit). Frames outside of trials (teleport period) are excluded from dF/F.\n- Track is 450 cm; reward zones are 50 cm wide at A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370].\n- On switch days, reward zone changes on trial 31 (index 30).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/dandiset.yaml` + 11 subject directories `sub-mXX/` (m3, m4, m7, m11, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19).\n- One NWB file per session: `sub-<mouse>_ses-<day>_behavior+ophys.nwb` (152 files, 87 GB). `ses-NN` = experiment day (1-14); m11 has days 3-14 only (12 sessions; imaging started day 3 due to low expression - consistent with methods).\n- NWB contents (read with `pynwb.NWBHDF5IO`):\n  - `acquisition/TwoPhotonSeries`: placeholder (1,1,1) - raw movie not included.\n  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries`: time series sampled **1:1 with imaging frames** (dt = 0.06448363 s = 15.5078 Hz, identical in every session), each with explicit `timestamps`:\n    - `position` (cm, -500 before scan start), `speed` (cm/s), `lick` (cumulative count per frame), `reward_zone` (reward-zone entry, cumulative per frame), `autoreward`, `environment` (-1 pre-scan, 0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2), `trial number`, `trial_start` (binary), `teleport` (binary), `scanning` (1 = scanning), and `Reward` (74 events; **timestamps** of reward delivery, data = volume mL).\n  - `processing/ophys`:\n    - `Fluorescence/planeN`: raw ROI F, shape (n_frames, n_rois), `rate` = 15.5078 (single-plane) or 31.0156 (2-plane, but each plane series still has one sample per behavior frame, i.e. 15.5 Hz per plane).\n    - `Neuropil/planeN`: Fneu.\n    - `Deconvolved/planeN`: suite2p `spks` computed from **raw F** (NOT the paper's dF/F-based events; correlation with my recomputed events ~0.5, see Step 4).\n    - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation`: columns `pixel_mask`/`voxel_mask`, `iscell` (n_rois x 2: [iscell flag, probability]), `planeIdx`.\n  - `subject`: subject_id (m3 ...), sex, DOB. `identifier` encodes original path incl. **scene** name, e.g. `/data/InVivoDA/GCAMP11/23_02_2023/Env1_LocationB_to_A`.\n- No `trials` intervals table: trial structure must be derived from `trial_start` / `teleport` binary series (as in the reference `sess.trial_start_inds` / `sess.teleport_inds`).\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 152 (11 subjects; 14 each except m11 = 12) |\n| ROIs total / iscell total | 336,545 / **138,678** |\n| iscell neurons per session | mean 912, min 155, max 2341 |\n| Subjects | 11 |\n| Sessions / subject | 12-14 |\n| Trials (total) | 12,216 |\n| Trials / session | mean 80.4, sd 6.2, min 41, max 100 |\n| Frames per session | 9,401-43,142 on-track (total on-track = 2,620,514; 72.6% of all frames) |\n| Frame period | 0.06448363 s (15.5078 Hz), identical in all sessions |\n| Planes | 1 plane except m17, m18 (2 planes, pooled) |\n| Mean trial length | 217 frames (14.0 s) |\n| Lick-sensor-error trials (>30% frames with cum. lick > 2) | **81** |\n| Omission fraction (per session) | mean 0.153 (range 0.037-0.30) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons / session | 155-2172 | \"yielded 155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" |\n| Subjects (switch task) | 11 | \"performing reward switches (n = 11 mice)\" |\n| Trials / session | 80.5 +- 7.4 | \"mean +- s.d., 80.5 +- 7.4 trials across 14 mice, all imaging days\" |\n| Total imaged trials | 12,376 (11 switch mice) | \"n = 81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice\" |\n| Lick-error trials removed | 81 (~0.65%) | same quote |\n| Neural sampling | ~15.5 Hz (0.0645 s) per plane | \"unidirectional scanning at ~15.5 Hz\"; \">30% of the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples\" |\n| Interneuron exclusion | dF/F vs speed Pearson r > 0.5; 0.42 +- 0.85% of cells | \"Additional putative interneurons were detected for exclusion ... by a Pearson correlation of >0.5 between their dF/F timeseries and the animal's running speed\" |\n| Reward omission rate | ~15% | \"the reward was randomly omitted on ~15% of trials\" |\n| Track length | 450 cm | \"450 cm virtual linear track\" |\n| Reward zones | A 80-130, B 200-250, C 320-370 cm (50 cm wide) | \"zone A, 80-130 cm; zone B, 200-250 cm; zone C, 320-370 cm\" |\n| Reward switch | day 3, 5, 7, 8(+env change), etc., **at trial 31 (index 30)** | reference `get_reward_zones(change_trial=30)` |\n| Speed threshold for spatial analyses | 2 cm/s | \"we excluded activity when the animal was moving at <2 cm/s\" |\n| Spatial bins | 45 bins of 10 cm | \"binned the 450 cm linear track into 45 bins of 10 cm each\" |\n| Sessions used for decoding | 77 (11 mice, 7 switch days) | \"n = 77 sessions, 11 mice, seven switch days\" |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **dF/F**: per trial (teleport periods excluded), neuropil subtracted (0.7 coefficient, trial-mean neuropil added back), baseline by **maximin** (gaussian smooth sigma = 15 frames -> 20 s min filter (300 frames) -> 300-frame max filter), dF/F = (F - F0)/|F0|, then Gaussian smoothing with 2-sample s.d. (~0.129 s).\n- **Events**: OASIS deconvolution of the smoothed dF/F (suite2p `dcnv.oasis`, tau = 0.7, rate = 15.5078 Hz/plane). \"not interpreted as a spike rate but rather as a method to eliminate the asymmetric smoothing of the calcium signal\".\n- **Trials**: from `trial_start` (track entry) to `teleport` (track exit). Teleport/ITI excluded.\n- **Alignment**: VR behavior already interpolated onto imaging frame times in the NWB files (one behavior sample per imaging frame).\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules**: suite2p `iscell` (manual curation removing multi-soma/dendrite/interneuron-like ROIs) + exclusion of putative interneurons with dF/F-speed Pearson r > 0.5.\n\n**Trial curation rules**: trials with erroneous lick detection (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) are set to NaN for lick analyses (81 of 12,376 trials).\n\n### Decoders Trained (paper)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| RR (reward-relative) circular position from deconvolved events of RR cells | \"decode score\" = mean cos(true - predicted); reported vs shuffle, z-scored; no classification accuracy reported. Positions with z-scored decode > 2 spanned -104.5 +- 20.1 cm to +152.7 +- 22.9 cm relative to reward |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Consistency checks run on the raw data (scripts in /app/cache)\n1. **Trial count**: 12,216 trials in the 152 NWB sessions vs 12,376 reported in the paper (11 switch mice). Mean 80.4 +- 6.2 trials/session vs paper 80.5 +- 7.4 (paper value is across 14 mice incl. 3 fixed-condition mice not in this DANDI release). Consistent.\n2. **Lick-sensor-error trials**: my implementation of the paper rule (>30% of frames in a trial with cumulative lick count > 2) flags **exactly 81 trials** across the whole dataset - identical to the paper's \"81 out of 12,376 trials removed\". Strong confirmation of the trial definition and lick variable interpretation.\n3. **Neurons/session**: iscell counts 155-2341; paper says 155-2172. The lower bound matches exactly; the upper bound differs because the paper counts the neurons actually analyzed after interneuron exclusion / per-day analysis sets (see Step 9 for the post-curation range).\n4. **Reward zones**: for each trial I took the animal position at the first `reward_zone` > 0 sample and matched it to the nearest zone start (A 80 / B 200 / C 320). This empirical zone agrees with the scene-name rule of `behavior.get_reward_zones` (switch at trial index 30) for **100% of trials in all 152 sessions**; maximum deviation of first-entry position from the nominal zone start is 8.5 cm (1 frame of running at ~80 cm/s = ~5 cm). Confirms both the zone dictionary and the change_trial = 30 convention.\n5. **Omission rate**: mean 15.3% per session (paper: ~15%).\n6. **Environment**: `environment` is constant within every trial in every session (0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2); on `EnvX_?_to_EnvY_?` days it switches exactly at trial index 30 together with the reward zone.\n7. **Interneuron exclusion**: recomputing dF/F and correlating with speed flags ~0.65% of iscell ROIs in the test session (paper: 0.42 +- 0.85% across mice/days).\n8. **NWB `Deconvolved` vs reference events**: the NWB Deconvolved series is suite2p `spks` derived from raw F, not from the paper's maximin dF/F (correlation with recomputed events ~0.25-0.7). **Decision: recompute dF/F and OASIS events following the reference code** rather than use the stored Deconvolved series.\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Trial index range for dF/F | `f[:, start-1:stop-1]` (off-by-one) | trial_start/teleport indices are on-track at [start, stop) | trials = track entry to teleport | Use [start, stop) consistently for neural and behavior; the reference's -1 offset would leave the final frame of each trial undefined. Difference is 1 frame (64 ms) in baseline estimation only. |\n| Deconvolved data source | reference deconvolves its own dF/F | NWB stores suite2p spks from raw F | events from dF/F | Recompute dF/F + OASIS as in reference |\n| Total trials | - | 12,216 | 12,376 | 160-trial difference: DANDI release lacks a few sessions (e.g. m11 days 1-2 were never imaged); no action |\n| Speed threshold (2 cm/s) | applied in place-cell/GLM analyses | - | \"excluded activity when moving <2 cm/s\" | NOT applied here: the decoder requires continuous time series and speed itself is a decoder output (with a <2 cm/s class). Documented deviation required by the decoding task. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source (NWB) | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `ophys/Fluorescence/planeN` (F), `ophys/Neuropil/planeN` (Fneu), `ImageSegmentation.iscell` | `neural` | per-trial dF/F (neuropil subtract 0.7 + trial-mean add-back, maximin baseline: gauss sigma 15 frames -> min filter 300 -> max filter 300, dF/F = (F-F0)/|F0|, gaussian smooth sigma 2 frames) then OASIS deconvolution (tau 0.7, 15.5078 Hz) = \"events\" | `preprocessing.dff` (+`utilities.multi_anim_sess` defaults, `suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis`) | Neurons = ROIs with iscell==1 minus putative interneurons (dF/F vs speed r > 0.5). Planes pooled. |\n| `behavior/trial_start`, `behavior/teleport` | trial segmentation | frames [trial_start, teleport) | `sess.trial_start_inds`, `sess.teleport_inds` | teleport/ITI excluded, as in reference dF/F (keep_teleports=False) |\n| frame timestamps | `input[0]` time_from_trial_start (s) | t - t[trial_start] | - | continuous, time-varying |\n| `behavior/environment` | `input[1]` environment | 0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2 (constant within trial; broadcast over time) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`) | verified constant within every trial |\n| trial index | `input[2]` trial_number | 0-based index within session (broadcast) | - | continuous per-trial |\n| previous trial reward outcome | `input[3]` prev_trial_outcome | 0 = omitted, 1 = rewarded (broadcast) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`isreward`) | first trial of session: set to 1 (mice always received 30 rewarded warm-up trials immediately before the imaging session; ~85% of trials are rewarded) |\n| `behavior/position` + per-trial reward zone | `output[0]` dist_to_reward_zone (7 bins) | signed distance to nearest point of the active 50 cm zone (0 inside), binned <-50 / [-50,-10) / [-10,0) / 0 / (0,10] / (10,50] / >50 | `glmUtils.create_design_matrix` (rel_pos), `behavior.get_reward_zones` | linear (not circular) distance, per Decoder Task spec |\n| `behavior/position` | `output[1]` position (5 bins) | 90 cm bins over 0-450 cm | trial matrices (10 cm bins) in reference | positions clipped to [0,450] |\n| `behavior/speed` | `output[2]` speed (5 bins) | <2 / 2-10 / 10-20 / 20-40 / >40 cm/s | `sess.vr_data['speed']` | the 2 cm/s reference threshold appears as class boundary |\n| `behavior/lick` | `output[3]` lick (binary) | cumulative count per frame -> `lick>0 -> 1` | `behavior.lickrate`, `glmUtils` (`licks[licks>1]=1`) | trials flagged by sensor-error rule are dropped (see decisions) |\n| scene name in `identifier` (+ switch at trial 30), verified vs `behavior/reward_zone` entry position | `output[4]` reward_zone_location | A=0, B=1, C=2 (broadcast) | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | |\n| `behavior/Reward` timestamps + `behavior/reward_zone` | `output[5]` reward_outcome | 1 if a reward was delivered AND the reward zone was entered within the trial, else 0 (broadcast) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`isreward`) | |\n| `nwb.subject.subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | m3...m19 | - | 11 mice |\n| `imaging_plane.location` = \"hippocampus, CA1\" | `brain_regions` = ['CA1'], `brain_region_idx` | all neurons -> 0 | - | single region |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neural signal = deconvolved events from the paper's dF/F** (not the NWB `Deconvolved` series): the NWB Deconvolved is suite2p spks from raw F, whereas all reference analyses (place cells, decoding, GLM) use events deconvolved from the maximin dF/F. Recomputing follows `preprocessing.dff(..., deconvolve=True)` exactly (neu_coef 0.7, maximin, 2-frame smoothing, OASIS tau 0.7).\n2. **Temporal resolution = native imaging frame period (64.484 ms)**: behavior in the NWB is already interpolated onto imaging frames 1:1 (the reference's `vr_align_to_2P` alignment), so no rebinning is needed and no alignment error is introduced. Identical dt in every session satisfies the \"same bin size everywhere\" requirement.\n3. **Trial window = [trial_start, teleport)** (track entry to track exit), matching the reference definition of a trial; the ITI/teleport period is excluded exactly as in the reference dF/F (`keep_teleports=False`). I use [start, stop) rather than the reference's [start-1, stop-1) indexing so that neural and behavior frames are consistently aligned and no frame is left undefined (1-frame difference, only affects baseline estimation).\n4. **Neuron curation**: `iscell == 1` (suite2p manual curation) AND dF/F-vs-speed Pearson r <= 0.5 (paper's putative-interneuron exclusion). Multi-plane animals: planes pooled (as in the paper, except Ext. Fig. 7).\n5. **Trial curation**: drop trials flagged by the paper's lick-sensor-error rule (>30% of frames in the trial with cumulative lick count > 2); these are exactly the 81 trials the paper removed and their lick output would otherwise be corrupt/NaN. All other trials kept (min length 96 frames = 6.2 s).\n6. **No speed thresholding**: the reference excludes <2 cm/s samples for spatial tuning analyses, but the decoder requires continuous time series and speed is itself a decoded variable (with a <2 cm/s class); dropping those samples would destroy the alignment and the speed output. Documented deviation.\n7. **Per-trial variables are broadcast across time** so that `input` is (4, T) and `output` is (6, T) - the format allows mixing only if all entries in a trial array share the shape.\n8. **First trial's previous outcome = 1 (rewarded)**: the imaging session is always preceded by 30 warm-up trials with the same reward zone; treating the unobserved previous trial as rewarded matches the modal outcome (~85%).\n9. **Alignment event = trial start (track entry)**; `off_start = 0.0`; `off_end = None` because trials have variable length (median ~14 s).\n10. **10 sessions (m17/m18) have ophys arrays 1 frame longer than the behavior series**; the extra trailing frame is after the last teleport, so ophys is truncated to the behavior length.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Trials: total 12,216 - 81 lick-error = 12,135 trials; per session 41-100 (mean ~80).\n- [ ] Neurons: 152 sessions, per-session count in the paper's 155-2172 range after curation; interneuron exclusion ~0.4-0.7% of cells.\n- [ ] Subjects: 11; sessions per subject 12-14.\n- [ ] Reward outcome fraction ~0.85 (omission ~15%).\n- [ ] Reward zone labels: distribution across A/B/C; verified per trial against the observed reward-zone entry position (100% agreement in Step 4).\n- [ ] dist_to_reward_zone == 0 exactly when position is inside the active zone; bin 3 occupancy > 50/450 (animals slow in the zone).\n- [ ] Position bins approximately monotonic in time within a trial (unidirectional track).\n- [ ] Speed bin distribution: most samples 10-40 cm/s; <2 cm/s class present.\n- [ ] Lick fraction ~2-10% of frames.\n- [ ] Independent spot-check (Step 10) recomputing neural/input/output values for specific (session, trial, neuron, timepoint) directly from the NWB file with `np.allclose`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the Step 5 mapping. per-trial loops are only over trials (~80), all cell operations are vectorised; interneuron correlation is a single matrix-vector product instead of a per-cell loop.\n\nCode speedups added: multiprocessing over sessions (12 workers, `maxtasksperchild=1` to release memory), float32 arrays, `np.searchsorted` for reward frame indices, vectorised correlation.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nRan `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing` (sessions m11 day 3 and m3 day 5) then `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only`.\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Neurons (total) | 1,173 (154 + 1,019) |\n| Neurons / session | 154, 1019 (iscell 155, 1022; What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it. Reward event timestamps are mapped to the nearest behavior timepoint using `searchsorted`. For each trial, the output is 1 if any reward event occurred within the trial, 0 otherwise. The value is constant across all timepoints in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nerr = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\nassert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n...\noutput_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The alignment error is checked to be within half a time bin. `np.any` is used because a trial either was rewarded or not — the specific timepoint doesn't matter for the per-trial binary output.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n\n## 13-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\\n- Use `pynwb` to interface with the data. Do not directly load and parse the `hdf5` files with `h5py`.\\n- Write efficient code:\\n  - Vectorize loops\\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\\n\\n**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\\n\\n**\\u26a0\\ufe0f DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\\n\\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \\n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \\n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \\n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \\n3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\\n4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\\n- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 7\\\"\\n\\n**\\u26a0\\ufe0f DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\\n\\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \\n5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \\n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \\n7. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \\n\\n**Done when**: \\n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 11\\\" with a table of accuracies\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\\n\\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \\n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \\n\\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \\n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \\n\\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \\n\\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\\n2. Check temporal alignment \\u2014 plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \\n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\n**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 12\\\".\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\\n\\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\\n  - Key decisions and rationale\\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\\n  - Validation results and tables\\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\\n- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\\n  - Dataset description\\n  - How to load and use the converted data\\n  - Output format specification\\n  - Key statistics\\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\\n- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\\n\\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\\n\\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0.
