Use `pynwb` to load data files.\\n2. Match the target data structure exactly\\n3. Preserve all relevant information from the source\\n4. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\\n5. Match information provided in the reference texts\\n6. Include complete and accurate metadata\\n7. Be reproducible with documented code\\n8. Each decision about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. The `perms` or shuffle information were not saved\\r\\non figshare to reduce filesize.\\r\\n* `is_reward` refers to a 1 x N trials binary array of whether a given trial was rewarded or not (1=rewarded, 0=omission trial)\\r\\n* `morph` is a 1 x N trials binary array of which environment was active (0=Env 1, 1=Env 2)\\r\\n* `dream` is something unused from another experiment, ignore it (it will be all zeros)\\r\\n* `rzone` is a 2 x N trials array of the reward zone start and end coordinates, in cm\\r\\n* `rz label` is a 1 x N trials array with text labels for which reward zone was active ('A', 'B', 'C')\\r\\n* `trial dict` is a dictionary with entries `trial_set0` and `trial_set1`, which are each boolean arrays indicating which trials were included in which trial se\\r\\nt.\\r\\n\\r\\nWithin multi_anim_sess['GCAMP4']['sess'], there are attributes:\\r\\n```\\r\\ndict_keys(['vr_data', 'trial_start_inds', 'teleport_inds', 'scan_info', 'n_planes', 'timeseries', * 'F' is the raw fluorescence of each cell ROI\\r\\n    * 'Fneu' is the fluorescence in a small \\\"donut\\\" around the ROI that is subtracted in preprocessing before you get \\\"dff\\\"\\r\\n    * 'dff' is delta F/F\\r\\n    * 'events' is the deconvolved spike-like activity from 'dff' (but it's not really a spike rate, it just gets rid of the asymmetric smoothing of the calcium\\r\\nkernel)\\r\\n    * 'licks' are a cumulative lick count in each frame, so in my behavior code anything >1 gets set to 1 to be able to convert it accurately to a rate when I w\\r\\nant to convert it to Hz per position bin or time bin\\r\\n    * 'rewards' is just a binary at the sample where reward was delivered\\r\\n    * 'speed' is an actual smoothed running speed in cm/s for each sample.\\r\\n* `trial_matrices` are the spatially binned versions of timeseries, in bins of 10 cm, from 0 to 450 cm (not including the teleport zone). Shape: trials x positi\\r\\non bins x cells.\\r\\n* `trial_start_inds` is a 1 x N trials array of the sample index for the start of each trial (i.e. the start of the track)\\r\\n* `teleport_indices` is a 1 x N trials array of the sample index for the start of the teleport period (i.e. the end of the track)\\r\\n* `vr_data` is a Pandas dataframe of VR behavior data converted from the sqlite files output by Unity and already synchronized with the imaging data\\r\\n\\r\\nThe sess class, which aligns the VR data to the imaging data, is generated by [preprocessing.vr_align_to_2P](https://github.com/GiocomoLab/TwoPUtils/blob/00b658\\r\\n9e5afea4cfe2347557b0a2450d9673c435/TwoPUtils/preprocessing.py#L124) in the [TwoPUtils](https://github.com/GiocomoLab/TwoPUtils/tree/main) repo. ##\\r\\n\\r\\nfrom scipy.linalg import cho_factor, cho_solve\\r\\nfrom sklearn.base import BaseEstimator\\r\\n\\r\\nclass CircularRegression(BaseEstimator):\\r\\n\\r\\n    def __init__(self, alpha=0.0, tol=1e-5, max_iter=100):\\r\\n        self.alpha = alpha\\r\\n        self.tol = tol\\r\\n        self.max_iter = max_iter\\r\\n\\r\\n    def fit(self, X, y):\\r\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n        Parameters\\r\\n        ----------\\r\\n        X : array\\r\\n            Independent variables, has shape (n_timepoints x n_neurons)\\r\\n        y : array\\r\\n            Circular dependent variable, has shape (n_timepoints x 1),\\r\\n            all data should lie on the interval [-pi, +pi].\\r\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Convert 1d circular variable to 2d representation\\r\\n        u = np.column_stack([np.sin(y), np.cos(y)])\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Randomly initialize weights. Ensure scaling does\\r\\n        W = np.random.randn(X.shape[1], 2)\\r\\n        W /= np.max(np.sum(np.abs(X @ W), axis=1))\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Cache neuron x neuron gram matrix. This is used below\\r\\n        # in the M-step to solve a linear least squares problem\\r\\n        # in the form inv(XtX) @ XtY. Add regularization term to\\r\\n        # the diagonal.\\r\\n        XtX = X.T @ X\\r\\n        XtX[np.diag_indices_from(XtX)] += self.alpha\\r\\n        XtX = cho_factor(XtX)\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Compute model prediction in 2d space, and projection onto\\r\\n        # each observed u.\\r\\n        XW = (X @ W)\\r\\n        t = np.sum(u * XW, axis=1)\\r\\n        tcdf = normcdf(t)\\r\\n        tpdf = normpdf(t)\\r\\n\\r\\n        self.log_like_hist_ = [\\r\\n            np.log(2 * np.pi) -\\r\\n            0.5 * np.mean(np.sum(XW * XW, axis=1), axis=0) +\\r\\n            np.mean(np.log(1 + t * tcdf / tpdf))\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n\\r\\n        for itr in range(self.max_iter):\\r\\n\\r\\n            # E-step.\\r\\n            m = t + (tcdf / ((tpdf + t * tcdf)))\\r\\n            XtY = X.T @ (m[:, None] * u)\\r\\n\\r\\n            # M-step.\\r\\n            W = cho_solve(XtX, XtY)\\r\\n\\r\\n            # Recompute model prediction.\\r\\n            XW = X @ W\\r\\n            t = np.sum(u * XW, axis=1)\\r\\n            tcdf = normcdf(t)\\r\\n            tpdf = normpdf(t)\\r\\n\\r\\n            # Store log-likelihood.\\r\\n            self.log_like_hist_.append(\\r\\n                np.log(2 * np.pi) -\\r\\n                0.5 * np.mean(np.sum(XW * XW, axis=1), axis=0) +\\r\\n                np.mean(np.log(1 + t * tcdf / tpdf))\\r\\n            )\\r\\n\\r\\n            # Check convergence.\\r\\n            if (self.log_like_hist_[-1] - self.log_like_hist_[-2]) < self.tol:\\r\\n                break\\r\\n\\r\\n        self.weights_ = W\\r\\n\\r\\n    def predict(self, X):\\r\\n        u_pred = X @ self.weights_\\r\\n        return np.arctan2(u_pred[:, 0], u_pred[:, 1])\\r\\n\\r\\n    def score(self, X, y):\\r\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n        Returns 1 minus mean angular similarity between y and model prediction.\\r\\n\\r\\n        score == 1 for perfect prediction\\r\\n        score == 0 in expectation for random prediction\\r\\n        score == -1 if prediction is off by 180 degrees.\\r\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n        y_pred = self.predict(X)\\r\\n        return np.mean(np.cos(y - y_pred))\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef train_vs_test_blocks(X, y, VR provided tight control over s\\r\\nensory stimuli and the animal\\u2019s trajectory, allowing us to observe neuronal remapping in relation to distant rewards, a phenomenon potentially less visible in f\\r\\nreely moving scenarios. Furthermore, the task included multiple updates to a hidden reward zone across two environments, allowing us to dissociate spatially dri\\r\\nven and reward-driven remapping. Imaging began on day\\u20091 of task acquisition, in which animals traversed a unidirectional 450\\u2009cm virtual linear track (for exampl\\r\\ne, Environment 1 (ENV 1)) with a hidden 50\\u2009cm reward zone where sucrose water was delivered operantly for licking (Fig. 1d,e). On day\\u20093, the reward zone was mov\\r\\ned within a \\u2018switch\\u2019 session to a new track location, signaled by automatic reward delivery on the first ten post-switch trials only if the mouse failed to lick\\r\\n in the new zone. On day\\u20095, the reward zone was moved to a third location, then returned to its original location on day\\u20097. On day\\u20098, the reward switch coincide\\r\\nd with the introduction of a novel environment (for example, ENV 2), in which the switch order was then reversed (Fig. 1e). On \\u2018stay\\u2019 days, the last reward loca\\r\\ntion from the previous day was maintained. A separate \\u2018fixed-condition\\u2019 group of mice (n\\u2009=\\u20093) experienced only one reward location in a single environment (ENV\\r\\n1) (Extended Data Fig. 1), to control for the effect of experience. To maintain engagement, the reward was randomly omitted on ~15% of trials. At the end of the\\r\\n track, mice passed through a variable length gray \\u2018teleport\\u2019 zone (~50\\u2009cm\\u2009+\\u2009temporal jitter up to 10\\u2009s; Methods) before starting the next lap.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Behavioral training and VR tasks\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pre-training\\r\\n\\r\\nMice were pre-trained on a \\u2018running\\u2019 task on a 350\\u2009cm virtual linear track with random black and white checkerboard walls and a white floor to collect a cued re\\r\\nward. The reward location was marked by two large gray towers, initially positioned 50\\u2009cm down the track. The mouse was rewarded for licking within 25\\u2009cm of the\\r\\n towers, otherwise, an automatic reward was given at the towers. The towers then disappeared and quickly reappeared further down the track. Covering the distanc\\r\\ne to the current reward in under 20\\u2009s increased the inter-reward distance by 10\\u2009cm, but if the mouse took longer than 30\\u2009s, the distance decreased by 10\\u2009cm, wit\\r\\nh a maximum reward location of 300\\u2009cm. Upon consistent completion of 300\\u2009cm distance in under 20\\u2009s, the automatic reward was turned off, requiring the mice to l\\r\\nick to receive reward. Both environments consisted of a 450\\u2009cm linear track, with two colored towers (green in ENV 1, blue in ENV 2) and two patterne\\r\\nd towers along the walls. Excitation was achieved using 920\\u2009nm light from a tunable output femtosecond pulsed laser (Coherent Chameleon Discovery).\\r\\n Laser power modulation was accomplished with a Pockels cell (Conoptics). The typical excitation power, measured at the front face of the objective (Leica \\u00d725,\\r\\n1.0\\u2009NA, 2.6\\u2009mm working distance), ranged from 15\\u201368\\u2009mW for mice m2\\u2013m7, 50\\u2013100\\u2009mW for m10 and m11, and 13\\u201340\\u2009mW for m12\\u201319. The imaging field of view (FOV) was collected at\\r\\n 1.0 magnification with unidirectional scanning at ~15.5\\u2009Hz, resulting in an approximately 0.7\\u2009\\u00d7\\u20090.7\\u2009mm FOV (512\\u2009\\u00d7\\u2009796 pixels). In all mice, the same FOV was acquired each session by aligning to a reference image from p\\r\\nrevious days before the start of data acquisition, with the aid of the \\u2018searchref\\u2019 plugin in Scanbox. This allowed us to track single cells across days.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Calcium data processing\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Suite2P software package (v.0.10.3) was used to perform xy motion correction (rigid and non-rigid) and identify putative cell regions of interest (ROIs). Ma\\r\\nnual curation eliminated ROIs containing multiple somata or dendrites, lacking visually obvious transients, suspected of overexpressing the calcium indicator or\\r\\n exhibiting high and continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons. This approach yielded 155\\u20132172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\\r\\n, owing to variation in imaging window implant quality and viral expression. In multi-plane imaged animals (see \\u20182P imaging\\u2019), ROIs were identified separately p\\r\\ner plane, but planes were pooled for all analyses except those in Extended Data Fig. 7. Custom code was used to follow individual ROIs across imaging sessions u\\r\\nsing the intersection over union of their pixels. The threshold for ROI matching was chosen algorithmically for each dataset such that the intersection over uni\\r\\non for the best match for an ROI pair was always greater than the intersection over union for any second-best match.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo compute the \\u0394F/F (dF/F) for each ROI, baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20\\u2009s sliding wind\\r\\now. Limitation to individual trials both accounts for potential photobleaching over the session and avoids the teleport periods for sessions during which the la\\r\\nser power was reduced (see \\u20182P imaging\\u2019). The value of dF/F was then calculated for each cell as the fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute va\\r\\nlue of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129\\u2009s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calc\\r\\nium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p. We note that this deconvolution is not interpreted as a spike rate but rather as a method to eliminate\\r\\nthe asymmetric smoothing of the calcium signal induced by indicator kinetics. Additional putative interneurons were detected for exclusion from further analysis\\r\\n by a Pearson correlation of >0.5 between their dF/F timeseries and the animal\\u2019s running speed, excluding 0.42\\u2009\\u00b1\\u20090.85% of cells (mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d. across mice and day\\r\\ns).\\r\\n\\r\\nroot@3d76ecaec900:/app#\\r\\n\"\n/logs/agent/trajectory.json:1270:            \"content\": \"New Terminal Output:\\n and day\\r\\ns).\\r\\n\\r\\nroot@3d76ecaec900:/app# sed -n 39,61p /app/methods.txt\\r\\n# Estimation of anatomical location per ROI\\r\\n\\r\\nAP and ML ROI coordinates were computed as the 2D center of mass of ROI pixels, relative to each FOV. In multi-plane imaged animals, ROIs were separated accordi\\r\\nng to their deep or superficial imaging plane. To estimate deep-superficial (dorso-ventral (DV)) coordinates in single-plane animals, we acquired a z-stack scan\\r\\n after the day\\u200914 task session that extended \\u2212100\\u2009\\u00b5m below (\\u221260\\u2009\\u00b5m below in one mouse) to +100\\u2009\\u00b5m above the FOV in the DV axis, in 2\\u2009\\u00b5m steps with ~50 frames pe\\r\\nr step. Each step was independently motion-corrected as described above, and the mean of each step was used to compile the 3D z-stack array. We then took the AP\\r\\n projection of each ML slice of the z-stack (that is, looking at the side view of the z-stack). We minimum-filtered (4\\u2009\\u00d7\\u20090 pixel window, ML\\u2009\\u00d7\\u2009DV) and smoothed (\\r\\n2\\u2009\\u00d7\\u20092 pixel s.d. Gaussian) each slice, then detected the most prominent dip in brightness in the DV axis to approximate the location of cell bodies, under the a\\r\\nssumption that the GCaMP signal is typically absent in the nuclei and brighter in the surrounding neuropil. This yielded a per-pixel estimate of the depth of th\\r\\ne pyramidal layer center from the dorsal surface of the z-stack, which we then smoothed (40\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200940 pixel s.d. Gaussian) to approximate the cell layer \\u2018curvature\\u2019\\r\\nacross the FOV. The curvature estimate was registered to the mean FOV of each imaging session. Given that the imaging plane transects this curvature, calculatin\\r\\ng the mean DV distance of each ROI (across pixels) from the curvature provides an estimate of where each ROI resides in the deep-to-superficial axis.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Quantification of licking behavior\\r\\n\\r\\nThe capacitive lick sensor allowed us to detect single licks. A very small number of trials with erroneous lick detection from damage to the circuit were remove\\r\\nd from subsequent licking analysis by setting these values to NaN (~0.65% of all imaged trials, n\\u2009=\\u200981 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice). Thes\\r\\ne trials were detected by >30% of the 0.0645\\u2009s imaging frame samples in the trial containing a cumulative lick count >2, as this would have produced a sustained\\r\\n lick rate \\u226520\\u2009Hz. Remaining lick counts were converted to a binary vector and spatially binned at the same resolution as neural activity (10\\u2009cm bins), then div\\r\\nided by the time occupancy in each bin to yield a lick rate. We quantified licking precision over blocks of ten trials using an anticipatory lick ratio, compute\\r\\nd as:\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\rm{Lick}\\\\; \\\\rm{ratio}=\\\\frac{\\\\rm{Lic}\\\\rm{k}_{\\\\rm{in}}-\\\\rm{Lic}\\\\rm{k}_{\\\\rm{out}}}{\\\\rm{Lic}\\\\rm{k}_{\\\\rm{in}}+\\\\rm{Lic}\\\\rm{k}_{\\\\rm{out}}}\\r\\n\\r\\nwhere Lickin is the mean lick rate in a 50\\u2009cm \\u2018anticipatory\\u2019 zone before the start of the reward zone and Lickout is the mean rate outside of this zone and the\\r\\nreward zone. The reward zone itself is thus excluded to exclude consummatory licks (Extended Data Fig. 1e). A ratio of 1 indicates perfect licking only in the a\\r\\nnticipatory zone, a ratio of \\u22121 indicates licking only outside of this zone and a ratio of 0 indicates chance licking everywhere, excluding the reward zone.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Place cell identification\\r\\n\\r\\nFor all neural spatial activity analyses, we excluded activity when the animal was moving at <2\\u2009cm\\u2009s\\u22121. To obtain an activity matrix of trials\\u2009\\u00d7\\u2009position bins f\\r\\nor each cell, we binned the 450\\u2009cm linear track into 45 bins of 10\\u2009cm each. We took the mean dF/F or deconvolved calcium activity on each trial within each posi\\r\\ntion bin (note that taking the mean activity over time samples is equivalent to normalizing by the occupancy within a trial).\\r\\n\\r\\nWe defined place cells as cells with significant SI65 in either trial set 1 (pre-reward-switch) or trial set 2 (post-reward-switch) in a session. On days withou\\r\\nt a switch, we used the first and second halves of trials. For the SI, we used the deconvolved calcium activity as this reduces the asymmetry of the calcium sig\\r\\nnal. SI was calculated for calcium imaging as:\\r\\n\\r\\n{{\\\\rm{SI}}}=\\\\mathop{\\\\sum }\\\\limits_{i=1}^{N}{p}_{i}\\\\frac{{f}_{i}}{f}{\\\\rm{log}}_{2}\\\\frac{{f}_{i}}{f},\\r\\n\\r\\nwhere pi is the occupancy probability in position bin i for the whole session, fi is the trial-averaged activity per position bin i and f is the mean activity o\\r\\nver the whole session, computed as the sum of fi\\u2009\\u00d7\\u2009pi over all N bins. To determine pi, we calculated the per-trial occupancy (number of imaging samples) in eac\\r\\nh bin divided by the total number of samples per trial, then summed the occupancy probabilities across trials and divided by the total per session. To determine\\r\\n the significance of the SI scores, we created a null distribution by circularly permuting the position data relative to the timeseries of each cell, by a rando\\r\\nm amount between ~1\\u2009s and the length of the trial, independently on each trial. SI was calculated from the trial-averaged activity of each shuffle, and this shu\\r\\nffle procedure was repeated 100 times per cell. A cell\\u2019s true SI was considered significant if it exceeded 95% of the SI scores from all shuffles within an anim\\r\\nal, pooled across cells (more stringent than comparing to the shuffle of each individual cell). For plotting place cell firing over trials (for example, Fig. 2)\\r\\n, deconvolved calcium activity was normalized to the mean of each cell within a session and smoothed with a 10\\u2009cm\\u2009s.d. nansmooth1d(a, sig, axis=-1):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Gaussian smoothing that ignores NaNs (reward_relative.utilities.nansmooth).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_ = np.where(nan_inds, 0.0, a)\\n    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\\n    a_ = ndi.gaussian_filter1d(a_, sig, axis=axis)\\n    one = ndi.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\\n    return a_ / one\\n\\n\\ndef nansmooth_nd(a, sig):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"N-d version used by the reference for the baseline (TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\\n    a_ = one\\n\\n\\ndef scene_reward_zones(scene, ntrials, change_trial=CHANGE_TRIAL):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Per-trial reward zone label from the VR scene name.\\n\\n    Mirrors reward_relative.behavior.get_reward_zones: fixed-zone scenes keep one\\n    zone for the whole session; switch scenes (``..._X_to_..._Y``) use zone X for the\\n    first `change_trial` trials and zone Y afterwards.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    m = re.search(r'^Env\\\\d_Location([ABC])$', scene)\\n    if m:\\n        return np.array([m.group(1)] * ntrials)\\n    m = re.search(r'([ABC])_to_(?:Env\\\\d_)?(?:Location)?([ABC])$', scene)\\n    if m:\\n        n0 = min(change_trial, ntrials)\\n        return np.array([m.group(1)] * n0 + [m.group(2)] * (ntrials - n0))\\n    raise NotImplementedError(f'Reward zones not defined for scene {scene}')\\n\\n\\ndef empirical_reward_zones(pos, rzone, starts, teles):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Zone label per trial inferred from the animal position at reward-zone entry.\\n\\n    Returns an array with '?' for trials in which the zone was never triggered\\n    (omission trials).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    starts_cm = np.array([REWARD_ZONES[z][0] for z in 'ABC'])\\n    labels = []\\n    for a, b in zip(starts, teles):\\n        idx = np.where(rzone[a:b] > 0)[0]\\n        if len(idx) == 0:\\n            labels.append('?')\\n        else:\\n            p = pos[a:b][idx[0]]\\n            labels.append('ABC'[int(np.argmin(np.abs(starts_cm - p)))])\\n    return np.array(labels)\\n\\n\\ndef discretize_distance(d):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Distance (cm) to the reward zone -> 7 categories (see OUTPUT_VALUES[0]).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    out = np.full(d.shape, 3, dtype=np.int8)        # 3 == inside the zone (d == 0)\\n    out[d < -50] = 0\\n    out[(d >= -50) & (d < -10)] = 1\\n    out[(d >= -10) & (d < 0)] = 2\\n    out[(d > 0) & (d <= 10)] = 4\\n    out[(d > 10) & (d <= 50)] = 5\\n    out[d > 50] = 6\\n    return out\\n\\n\\ndef discretize_position(pos):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Position (cm) -> 5 equal bins spanning the 450 cm track.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    p = np.clip(pos, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH - 1e-9)\\n    return np.floor(p / 90.0).astype(np.int8)\\n\\n\\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\\n    return np.digitize(speed, [2.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0]).astype(np.int8)\\n\\n\\ndef signed_distance_to_zone(pos, zone_start, zone_end):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Signed distance (cm) to the nearest point of the reward zone; 0 inside.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    d = np.zeros_like(pos)\\n    before = pos < zone_start\\n    after = process_session(fn, show_processing=False, plot_dir='/app'):\\n    t0 = time.time()\\n    S = load_session(fn)\\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\\n\\n    starts, teles = S['starts'], S['teles']\\n    ntrials_raw = len(starts)\\n    fs = S['rate'] / S['n_planes']\\n\\n    # ---- neurons: suite2p curation\\n    keep = np.where(S['iscell'])[0]\\n    F = S['F'][keep]\\n    Fneu = S['Fneu'][keep]\\n\\n    t0 = time.time()\\n    dff, events = compute_dff_events(F, Fneu, starts, teles, fs)\\n    t_dff = time.time() - t0\\n\\n    # ---- neurons: exclude putative interneurons (corr(dF/F, speed) > 0.5)\\n    onmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\\n    sp = S['speed'][onmask]\\n    D = dff[:, onmask]\\n    Dz = D - D.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n    spz = sp - sp.mean()\\n    denom = (np.sqrt((Dz ** 2).sum(axis=1)) * np.sqrt((spz ** 2).sum()))\\n    corr_speed = np.where(denom > 0, (Dz @ spz) / np.maximum(denom, 1e-12), 0.0)\\n    is_interneuron = corr_speed > INTERNEURON_R\\n    cells = np.where(~is_interneuron)[0]\\n    n_interneurons = int(is_interneuron.sum())\\n\\n    # ---- per-trial behaviour variables\\n    pos, speed, lick = S['pos'], S['speed'], S['lick']\\n    rzone, env, trials (paper: 81/12,376 trials removed)\\n    lick_err = np.array([(lick[a:b] > LICK_ERROR_COUNT).mean() > LICK_ERROR_FRAC\\n                         for a, b in zip(starts, teles)])\\n\\n    # ---- Sucrose reward is delivered for licking in the\\n  zone and is randomly omitted on ~15% of trials. On switch days the zone moves on trial 31.\\n\\n## Processing\\n\\nNeural activity is the **deconvolved calcium event trace** computed exactly as in the paper's\\ncode (`reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff`):\\n\\n1. Keep only frames between `trial_start` and `teleport` (the inter-trial teleport period is excluded).\\n2. Subtract neuropil (coefficient 0.7) and add back the per-trial mean neuropil.\\n3. frame\\ntimes, so no resampling is performed and neural/behaviour streams are aligned frame by frame.\\n\\n## How to load\\n\\n```python\\nimport pickle\\nwith open('/app/converted_data.pkl', 'rb') as f:\\n    data = pickle.load(f)\\n\\nneural = data['neural'][session][trial]   # (n_neurons, T) float32, deconvolved events\\ninputs = data['input'][session][trial]    # (4, T) float32\\noutputs = data['output'][session][trial]  # (6, T) int8\\n```\\n\\nTo validate / train the reference decoder:\\n\\n```bash\\npython train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only\\npython /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing\\n```\\n\\n## Format specification\\n\\n| Key | Contents |\\n|---|---|\\n| `neural` | list of 152 sessions -> list of trials -> `(n_neurons, T)` float32 deconvolved events |\\n| `input` | list of sessions -> list of trials -> `(4, T)` float32 |\\n| `output` | list of sessions -> list of trials -> `(6, T)` int8 categorical |\\n| `subjects` | `['m3','m4','m7','m11','m12','m13','m14','m15','m17','m18','m19']` |\\n| `subject_idx` | `(152,)` int index into `subjects` |\\n| `brain_regions` | `['CA1']` |\\n| `brain_region_idx` | per session `(n_neurons,)` int array (all zeros) |\\n| `input_names` | `time_from_trial_start_s`, `environment`, `trial_number`, `prev_trial_rewarded` |\\n| `output_names` | `dist_to_reward_zone`, `position`, `speed`, `lick`, `reward_zone_location`, `reward_outcome` |\\n| `output_values` | class labels for each output (see below) |\\n| `metadata` | task description, `time_bin_size` = 64.484 ms, alignment event, per-session info |\\n\\n### Inputs\\n| # | Name | Type | Description |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 0 | `time_from_trial_start_s` | continuous, time-varying | seconds since track entry |\\n| 1 | `environment` | binary, per trial (broadcast) | 0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2 |\\n| 2 | `trial_number` | continuous, per trial (broadcast) | 0-based lap index within the session |\\n| 3 | `prev_trial_rewarded` | binary, per trial (broadcast) | previous lap rewarded (1) or omitted (0); 1 for the first lap |\\n\\n### Outputs\\n| # | Name | Classes |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| 0 | `dist_to_reward_zone` | 0: < -50 cm, 1: -50 to -10, 2: -10 to <0, 3: inside the zone (0), 4: >0 to +10, 5: +10 to +50, 6: > +50 cm |\\n| 1 | `position` | 0: <90, 1: 90-180, 2: 180-270, 3: 270-360, 4: >360 cm |\\n| 2 | `speed` | 0: <2, 1: 2-10, 2: 10-20, 3: 20-40, 4: >40 cm/s |\\n| 3 | `lick` | 0: no lick, 1: lick in that frame |\\n| 4 | `reward_zone_location` | 0: A, 1: B, 2: C (per trial, broadcast) |\\n| 5 | `reward_outcome` | 0: omitted, 1: rewarded (per trial, broadcast) |\\n\\nTrials are aligned to **trial start** (entry to the track at 0 cm); `off_start = 0`,\\n`off_end = None`
